# List 6

## Unit 1

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>creep</b></td><td>/kriːp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>缓慢地行进</td><td>creep along slowly<br>缓慢地爬行前进</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Traffic crept forward only a few meters at a time all morning.  整个上午车流每次只缓慢前进几米。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 drag</td><td>/ˈdræɡ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>累赘，阻力；拖曳</td><td>a drag on growth<br>增长的阻力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 High debt became a drag on the country's economy.  高额债务成了该国经济的阻力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 loiter</td><td>/ˈlɔɪtər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>闲荡，徘徊</td><td>loiter near the entrance<br>在入口附近闲荡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Teenagers loitered outside the shop until the owner finally complained.  少年们在店外闲荡，直到店主终于抱怨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tarry</td><td>/ˈtæri/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>逗留，耽搁</td><td>tarry no longer<br>不再逗留</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not tarry here; the last train leaves in minutes.  别在这儿磨蹭，末班车几分钟后就开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 scurry</td><td>/ˈskɜːri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>急匆匆地跑</td><td>scurry for cover<br>急忙找掩护</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Mice scurried across the floor when the light came on.  灯一亮，老鼠们急匆匆跑过地板。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 run</td><td>/rʌn/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>跑，奔跑；运转</td><td>run for the bus<br>跑着赶公交</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He ran three miles before breakfast every single morning.  他每天早餐前都跑三英里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>crescendo</b></td><td>/krəˈʃendoʊ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（渐强之后到达的）顶峰</td><td>reach a crescendo<br>达到顶峰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Complaints about the choking smoke rose to a deafening crescendo.  对令人窒息的烟雾的抱怨达到了震耳欲聋的顶点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apex</td><td>/ˈeɪˌpeks/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>顶点，最高点</td><td>the apex of his career<br>他事业的顶峰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 At the apex of her fame she suddenly left the stage.  在名声的顶峰，她突然离开了舞台。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 acme</td><td>/ˈækmi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>顶点，极点</td><td>the acme of perfection<br>完美的极致</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That performance marked the acme of her long career.  那场演出标志着她漫长职业生涯的顶峰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apogee</td><td>/ˈæpəˌdʒiː/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>最高点，极点</td><td>the apogee of power<br>权力的顶点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The empire reached its apogee under this remarkably able emperor.  这个帝国在这位极有才干的皇帝治下达到极盛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 climax</td><td>/ˈklaɪˌmæks/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>高潮，顶点</td><td>the climax of the story<br>故事的高潮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film builds slowly toward a violent and unexpected climax.  影片缓慢推向一个暴烈而意外的高潮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crest</td><td>/krest/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>顶部，浪尖，山顶</td><td>the crest of a wave<br>浪尖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The surfers waited patiently for the crest of the next big wave.  冲浪者们耐心等待下一个大浪的浪尖。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peak</td><td>/ˈpiːk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>顶峰，最高点</td><td>at the peak of<br>在…的顶峰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sales peaked in December and then fell sharply in January.  销售在十二月达到顶峰，一月急剧下滑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pinnacle</td><td>/ˈpɪnəkl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>顶峰，巅峰</td><td>the pinnacle of success<br>成功的顶峰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 At thirty she had reached the pinnacle of her profession.  三十岁时她已达到职业生涯的巅峰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 summit</td><td>/ˈsʌmɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>顶点</td><td>reach the summit<br>登上顶峰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The climbers reached the summit just before the storm closed in.  登山者在风暴逼近前登上顶峰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 zenith</td><td>/ˈzenɪθ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>最高点，巅峰</td><td>at the zenith of<br>在…的巅峰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The empire stood at the zenith of its power around that year.  大约在那一年，这个帝国正处于国力的巅峰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 nadir</td><td>/ˈneɪdɪr/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>最低点，谷底</td><td>reach the nadir<br>到达最低点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His confidence reached its nadir after the third rejection.  第三次被拒后他的信心降到了最低点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>crest</b></td><td>/krest/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>顶部，浪尖，山顶</td><td>the crest of a wave<br>浪尖</td></tr>

<tr><td>↳近 zenith</td><td>/ˈzenɪθ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>最高点，巅峰</td><td>at the zenith of<br>在…的巅峰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The empire stood at the zenith of its power around that year.  大约在那一年，这个帝国正处于国力的巅峰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bottom</td><td>/ˈbɑːtəm/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>底部，最低点</td><td>at the bottom<br>在底部</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their team finished at the bottom of the league again.  他们的球队又一次排在联赛末位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>crestfallen</b></td><td>/ˈkrestfɔːlən/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>垂头丧气的，沮丧的</td><td>look utterly crestfallen<br>显得十分沮丧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He came home crestfallen after losing the final match by one point.  决赛以一分之差落败后，他垂头丧气地回了家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 downcast</td><td>/ˈdaʊnˌkæst/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>沮丧的；低垂的</td><td>a downcast expression<br>沮丧的表情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He came home downcast after failing the driving test again.  他又一次没通过驾照考试，沮丧地回到家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dispirited</td><td>/dɪˈspɪrətɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>沮丧的，气馁的</td><td>a dispirited team<br>士气低落的队伍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dispirited workers returned to a factory nobody expected to survive.  气馁的工人回到一家谁都不看好的工厂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 low</td><td>/ˈloʊ/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>低落的，沮丧的；低的</td><td>feel a bit low<br>感觉有点低落</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has been feeling low since her closest friend moved away.  自从最好的朋友搬走，她一直情绪低落。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gloomy</td><td>/ˈɡluːmi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>忧郁的</td><td>a gloomy mood<br>忧郁的心情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She grew gloomy whenever the winter rain lasted more than a week.  每当冬雨连下一周以上，她就变得忧郁起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 melancholic</td><td>/ˌmelənˈkɑːlɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>忧郁的，伤感的</td><td>a melancholic mood<br>忧郁的心情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The melancholic music suited the grey afternoon outside perfectly.  这忧郁的音乐与窗外灰暗的午后十分相配。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sorrowful</td><td>/ˈsɑːroʊfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>悲伤的，忧伤的</td><td>a sorrowful farewell<br>悲伤的告别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her sorrowful eyes told us the news before she spoke.  她悲伤的眼睛在她开口前就告诉了我们消息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 woeful</td><td>/ˈwoʊfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>悲惨的，糟糕的</td><td>a woeful performance<br>糟糕的表现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team's woeful defending cost them the championship again.  球队糟糕的防守又一次让他们丢了冠军。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 elated</td><td>/ɪˈleɪtəd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>兴高采烈的，得意的</td><td>elated by the news<br>因消息而狂喜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was elated when the university finally accepted her application.  大学终于录取她时，她欣喜若狂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 buoyant</td><td>/ˈbuːjənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>心情好的</td><td>in a buoyant mood<br>心情愉快</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He arrived in a buoyant mood after hearing the good news.  听到好消息后，他心情愉快地到了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 excited</td><td>/ɪkˈsaɪtɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>兴奋的，激动的</td><td>excited about the trip<br>对旅行很兴奋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children were too excited to sleep before the trip.  孩子们太兴奋了，旅行前根本睡不着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 exhilarated</td><td>/ɪɡˈzɪləreɪtɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>兴奋的，欢欣的</td><td>exhilarated by the climb<br>因攀登而振奋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They felt exhilarated after skiing down the long open slope.  滑下那条长长的开阔雪道后，他们感到无比振奋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 exultant</td><td>/ɪɡˈzʌltənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>欢欣鼓舞的，得意的</td><td>an exultant crowd<br>欢腾的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Exultant fans filled the streets after the unexpected victory.  意外获胜后，欢腾的球迷挤满了街道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>cronyism</b></td><td>/ˈkroʊniɪzəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>任人唯亲，对好朋友的偏袒</td><td>accusations of cronyism<br>任人唯亲的指控</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor resigned amid loud accusations of cronyism in awarding city contracts.  市长在授予市政合同任人唯亲的强烈指控中辞职。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 favoritism</td><td>/ˈfeɪvərɪtɪzəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>偏爱，偏袒</td><td>show blatant favoritism<br>明显偏袒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Promoting his own nephew looked like blatant favoritism to everyone.  提拔自己的外甥在所有人看来都是明显的偏袒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>crook</b></td><td>/krʊk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使弯曲</td><td>crook one's finger<br>弯起手指</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She crooked her finger to call the waiter across the room.  她弯起手指，招呼房间另一头的服务员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arch</td><td>/ˈɑːrtʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>使成弓形；拱门</td><td>arch one's back<br>弓起背</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cat arched its back and hissed at the dog.  猫弓起背，对着狗嘶叫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bow</td><td>/ˈbaʊ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>使弯曲，鞠躬</td><td>bow one's head<br>低下头</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old branches bowed under the weight of wet snow.  老树枝在湿雪的重压下弯了下去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 curve</td><td>/ˈkɜːrv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>使弯曲；曲线</td><td>curve to the left<br>向左弯曲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The road curves sharply just beyond the stone bridge.  过了石桥不远，道路急剧弯曲。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hook</td><td>/ˈhʊk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>钩住，连接；钩子</td><td>hook the trailer on<br>把拖车挂上</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She hooked the two cables together before starting the engine.  她把两根电缆连接好才启动发动机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 swerve</td><td>/swɜːrv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>突然改变方向</td><td>swerve to avoid<br>急转以避开</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The driver swerved sharply to avoid a dog crossing the road.  司机猛地转向，避开横穿马路的狗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 straighten</td><td>/ˈstreɪtən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>使变直，弄整齐</td><td>straighten a wire<br>把铁丝弄直</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He straightened the bent nail carefully with a small hammer.  他用小锤子小心地把弯钉子弄直。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unbend</del></td><td>/ʌnˈbend/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使伸直，变直</td><td>unbend a paper clip<br>把回形针弄直</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She unbent the wire and used it to pick the lock.  她把铁丝弄直，用它撬开了锁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 uncurl</del></td><td>/ʌnˈkɜrl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>伸直，展开</td><td>uncurl its leaves<br>叶子展开</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fern slowly uncurled its new leaves in the spring sunshine.  蕨类在春日阳光下缓缓展开新叶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>croon</b></td><td>/kruːn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>低声吟唱，轻声哼唱</td><td>croon a lullaby<br>低声唱摇篮曲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mother crooned an old lullaby until the baby fell asleep.  母亲低声哼着一首老摇篮曲，直到婴儿睡去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 whisper</td><td>/ˈwɪspər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>低声说，耳语</td><td>whisper in her ear<br>在她耳边低语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She whispered the answer so the teacher would not hear.  她低声说出答案，以免老师听见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grumble</td><td>/ˈɡrʌmbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>（尤指低声地）抱怨，埋怨</td><td>grumble under one's breath<br>低声抱怨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The workers grumbled about the new rules but followed them anyway.  工人们低声抱怨新规定，但还是照办了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mumble</td><td>/ˈmʌmbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>说话含糊</td><td>mumble an apology<br>含糊地道歉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He mumbled an apology and hurried out of the crowded room.  他咕哝了一句道歉，匆匆走出拥挤的房间。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 murmur</td><td>/ˈmɜːrmər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>低声抱怨，发牢骚</td><td>murmur a complaint<br>低声抱怨一句</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The workers murmured about the new rules but nobody complained openly.  工人们对新规定低声抱怨，却没人公开抗议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 shout</td><td>/ˈʃaʊt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>喊叫，大声说</td><td>shout at someone<br>对某人大喊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please don't shout; I can hear you perfectly well.  请别喊，我听得很清楚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 yell</td><td>/ˈjel/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>大叫，叫喊</td><td>yell out a warning<br>大声发出警告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He yelled a warning just before the branch fell down.  就在树枝落下前他大声警告了一声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>culminate</b></td><td>/ˈkʌlmɪneɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>达到高潮；以…告终</td><td>culminate in victory<br>以胜利告终</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of quiet training culminated in an Olympic gold medal.  多年默默的训练最终以一枚奥运金牌告终。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cap</td><td>/ˈkæp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>限制，设上限</td><td>cap the spending<br>限制开支上限</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The government capped tuition increases at three percent this year.  政府将今年学费涨幅限制在百分之三。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 climax</td><td>/ˈklaɪˌmæks/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>高潮，顶点</td><td>the climax of the story<br>故事的高潮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film builds slowly toward a violent and unexpected climax.  影片缓慢推向一个暴烈而意外的高潮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crown</td><td>/ˈkraʊn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使达到顶点；王冠</td><td>crown a career<br>为职业生涯添彩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prize crowned a long career of quiet, patient research.  这个奖为漫长而耐心的研究生涯锦上添花。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>crucial</b></td><td>/ˈkruːʃl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常重要的，决定性的</td><td>a crucial decision<br>关键的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The next two weeks will be crucial to the project's survival.  接下来两周对这个项目的存亡至关重要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 critical</td><td>/ˈkrɪtɪkəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>关键的；批评的</td><td>a critical moment<br>关键时刻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Water supply is critical to the survival of this town.  供水对这座小镇的存续至关重要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 decisive</td><td>/dɪˈsaɪsɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>决定性的；果断的</td><td>a decisive victory<br>决定性的胜利</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her testimony was decisive in the trial.  她的证词在审判中起了决定性作用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 key</td><td>/ˈkiː/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>关键的；钥匙</td><td>a key factor<br>关键因素</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Trust is the key element in any long working relationship.  信任是任何长期工作关系中的关键要素。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pivotal</td><td>/ˈpɪvətl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>最关键的，最为重要的</td><td>play a pivotal role<br>起关键作用</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her testimony played a pivotal role in the outcome of the trial.  她的证词对审判结果起了关键作用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vital</td><td>/ˈvaɪtəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>充满活力的；极重要的</td><td>full of vital energy<br>充满生命活力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Regular exercise keeps older people vital and mentally alert.  经常锻炼使老年人保持活力和头脑清醒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inconsequential</td><td>/ˌɪnkɑːnsɪˈkwenʃəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不重要的，微不足道的</td><td>an inconsequential remark<br>无关紧要的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The difference between the two prices is quite inconsequential.  两个价格之间的差别微不足道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 insignificant</td><td>/ˌɪnsɪɡˈnɪfɪkənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关紧要的，微不足道的</td><td>a seemingly insignificant change<br>看似微小的改动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An insignificant error early on ruined the entire calculation.  早期一个微不足道的错误毁了整个计算。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 trivial</td><td>/ˈtrɪviəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无足轻重的，不重要的</td><td>a trivial detail<br>无关紧要的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor refused to argue over such a trivial difference in wording.  编辑不愿为措辞上如此无足轻重的差别争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>crumple</b></td><td>/ˈkrʌmpl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>弄皱，压皱；崩溃</td><td>crumple a piece of paper<br>把纸揉成一团</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He crumpled the rejection letter and threw it into the bin.  他把那封拒信揉皱，扔进了垃圾桶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rumple</td><td>/ˈrʌmpl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>打乱，使不整齐</td><td>rumple his hair<br>把他头发弄乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His aunt always rumpled the little boy's hair when she visited.  他姨妈每次来都会揉乱小男孩的头发。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 crimple</del></td><td>/ˈkrɪmpəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使起皱，弄皱</td><td>crimple the fabric<br>把布料弄皱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Careless packing crimpled the silk dress beyond any repair.  草率的打包把这条丝绸裙弄皱得没法补救。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crease</td><td>/ˈkriːs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>起皱，弄出折痕；折痕</td><td>crease the trousers<br>把裤子弄出折痕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sitting all day had creased his new linen jacket badly.  坐了一整天，他的新麻布外套皱得很厉害。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fold</td><td>/ˈfoʊld/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>折叠；褶痕</td><td>fold the laundry<br>叠洗好的衣服</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She folded the letter twice and slipped it into her pocket.  她把信折了两次，塞进口袋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wrinkle</td><td>/ˈrɪŋkəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>起皱；皱纹</td><td>wrinkle one's nose<br>皱起鼻子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The shirt wrinkles easily unless you hang it up immediately.  这件衬衫不马上挂起来就容易起皱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 smooth</td><td>/ˈsmuːð/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>温和的，圆滑的；平滑的</td><td>a smooth talker<br>说话圆滑的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is a smooth talker who never offends anyone in negotiations.  他谈判时言辞圆滑，从不得罪任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 2

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>crutch</b></td><td>/krʌtʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>拐杖；支柱，依靠</td><td>walk on crutches<br>靠拐杖走路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Humor became a crutch he leaned on whenever conversations turned serious.  每当谈话变得严肃，幽默就成了他依靠的支柱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brace</td><td>/ˈbreɪs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使振作，鼓励；支撑</td><td>brace yourself for impact<br>做好承受冲击准备</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A cup of hot tea braced him for the long night.  一杯热茶让他为漫长的夜晚振作起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 buttress</td><td>/ˈbʌtrɪs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>支持，加强（论点）</td><td>buttress an argument<br>为论点提供支撑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Fresh evidence buttressed the prosecutor's case against the two suspects.  新证据支持了检察官针对两名嫌疑人的指控。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 column</td><td>/ˈkɑːləm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>柱子，支柱；专栏</td><td>a stone column<br>石柱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two marble columns still support the roof of the ruined temple.  两根大理石柱仍支撑着这座残破神庙的屋顶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stay</td><td>/ˈsteɪ/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>支撑物，支柱；停留</td><td>a stay for the mast<br>桅杆的支索</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tent's stays snapped in the wind and everything collapsed.  帐篷的支索在风中断裂，一切都塌了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 support</td><td>/səˈpɔːrt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>支持，支撑</td><td>support a proposal<br>支持一项提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most members voted to support the new proposal.  大多数成员投票支持这项新提案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 underpinning</td><td>/ˈʌndərpɪnɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>支撑，基础</td><td>the underpinning of the theory<br>该理论的基础</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cheap credit was the underpinning of the whole housing boom.  廉价信贷是整场房产热的支撑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 underpropping</del></td><td>/ˌʌndərˈprɑpɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>支撑，支柱</td><td>the underpropping of the wall<br>墙体的支撑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Timber underpropping kept the damaged wall standing through the winter.  木质支撑让受损的墙撑过了整个冬天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>crux</b></td><td>/krʌks/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>中心，关键点</td><td>the crux of the matter<br>问题的关键</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crux of the dispute is who actually owns the water rights.  争端的关键是水权究竟归谁所有。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 core</td><td>/ˈkɔːr/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>核心，要点</td><td>the core of the argument<br>论点的核心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 At the core of the dispute lies a simple money question.  争端的核心是一个简单的金钱问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 substance</td><td>/ˈsʌbstəns/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>要旨，实质；物质</td><td>the substance of his speech<br>他讲话的要旨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The substance of her complaint was that nobody had asked her.  她抱怨的实质是没人问过她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 kernel</td><td>/ˈkɜːrnəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>核心，要点；果核</td><td>the kernel of truth<br>一点真实的核心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a kernel of truth in this otherwise absurd rumor.  这个荒唐的谣言里有一点真实的核心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gist</td><td>/dʒɪst/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>要点</td><td>get the gist of<br>抓住…的要点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She summarized the gist of the long report in only three sentences.  她只用三句话就概括了那份长报告的要点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pivot</td><td>/ˈpɪvət/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>核心，关键；支点</td><td>the pivot of the plan<br>计划的关键</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Trade was the pivot on which the whole treaty turned.  贸易是整个条约的关键所在。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 trifle</td><td>/ˈtraɪfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>轻视，玩弄；小事</td><td>trifle with her feelings<br>玩弄她的感情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not trifle with her feelings just to amuse yourself.  不要为了取乐而玩弄她的感情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 triviality</td><td>/ˌtrɪviˈæləti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>琐事；微不足道</td><td>argue over trivialities<br>为琐事争吵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The long meeting sank into arguments about pure trivialities.  漫长的会议陷入了对纯粹琐事的争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>cryptic</b></td><td>/ˈkrɪptɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>秘密的；难以理解的</td><td>a cryptic remark<br>费解的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His cryptic note gave us no hint about where he had gone.  他那张费解的便条没有透露他去了哪里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 covert</td><td>/ˈkoʊvərt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>隐蔽的，秘密的</td><td>covert operations<br>秘密行动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The agency ran covert operations in several neighboring countries.  该机构在数个邻国进行秘密行动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 furtive</td><td>/ˈfɜːrtɪv/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>鬼鬼祟祟的；秘密的</td><td>a furtive glance<br>偷偷的一瞥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He cast a furtive glance at the answers on her desk.  他偷偷瞥了一眼她桌上的答案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 secret</td><td>/ˈsiːkrət/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>秘密的，隐秘的；秘密</td><td>keep a secret<br>保守秘密</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They held secret meetings in the basement for several months.  他们在地下室秘密开会数月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stealthy</td><td>/ˈstelθi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>隐秘的，偷偷的</td><td>a stealthy approach<br>隐秘的接近</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cat made a stealthy approach toward the sleeping bird.  猫悄悄地靠近那只睡着的鸟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arcane</td><td>/ɑːrˈkeɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深奥的，难以理解的</td><td>arcane rules<br>晦涩难懂的规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tax code is full of arcane rules nobody understands.  税法充满无人理解的晦涩规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enigmatic</td><td>/ˌenɪɡˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>神秘的，难以理解的</td><td>an enigmatic smile<br>神秘的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her enigmatic answer left the reporters guessing for several days.  她神秘的回答让记者们猜了好几天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impenetrable</td><td>/ɪmˈpenɪtrəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以理解的</td><td>impenetrable jargon<br>晦涩难懂的行话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The contract was written in impenetrable jargon that confused everyone.  合同用晦涩难懂的行话写成，让所有人都困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inscrutable</td><td>/ɪnˈskruːtəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>高深莫测的，难以理解的</td><td>an inscrutable expression<br>高深莫测的表情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His inscrutable smile gave nothing away about his plans.  他那高深莫测的笑容丝毫没有透露他的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mystic</td><td>/ˈmɪstɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>神秘的，玄妙的</td><td>a mystic experience<br>神秘的体验</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old poem describes a mystic union with the natural world.  这首古诗描写了与自然界的神秘合一。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 occult</td><td>/əˈkʌlt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以理解的</td><td>occult knowledge<br>玄奥的知识</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manuscript is filled with occult knowledge that scholars still cannot decipher.  这份手稿满是学者至今无法解读的玄奥知识。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uncanny</td><td>/ʌnˈkæni/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>离奇的，奇异的</td><td>an uncanny resemblance<br>异乎寻常的相似</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The twins share an uncanny ability to finish each other's sentences.  这对双胞胎有一种离奇的本事，能接上对方的话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 public</td><td>/ˈpʌblɪk/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>公开的；公众的</td><td>make it public<br>予以公开</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company made its findings public earlier this month.  公司本月早些时候公开了调查结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 open</td><td>/ˈoʊpən/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>公开的，坦率的</td><td>open opposition<br>公开的反对</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was open opposition to the plan at the meeting.  会上有人公开反对这项计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 comprehensible</td><td>/ˌkɑːmpriˈhensəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可理解的，易懂的</td><td>clear and comprehensible<br>清晰易懂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Good teachers make difficult ideas comprehensible to complete beginners.  好老师能让初学者也理解艰深的概念。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 understandable</td><td>/ˌʌndərˈstændəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可理解的；情有可原的</td><td>perfectly understandable<br>完全可以理解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His anger was understandable given how badly he was treated.  考虑到他受到的糟糕对待，他的愤怒可以理解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>culpable</b></td><td>/ˈkʌlpəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>该受谴责的，有罪的</td><td>culpable negligence<br>应受责罚的疏忽</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report found two managers culpable for the safety failures.  报告认定两位经理对安全事故负有过失。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blameworthy</td><td>/ˈbleɪmˌwɜrði/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>该受责备的</td><td>blameworthy conduct<br>该受责备的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Both drivers were blameworthy, though only one was actually fined.  两位司机都该受责备，但只有一人被罚款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 censurable</del></td><td>/ˈsɛnʃərəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>该受谴责的</td><td>censurable behavior<br>该受谴责的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee called his handling of the funds clearly censurable.  委员会称他对资金的处理明显应受谴责。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 guilty</td><td>/ˈɡɪlti/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有罪的；内疚的</td><td>found guilty of fraud<br>被判欺诈罪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt guilty about forgetting his mother's birthday again.  他又忘了母亲的生日，心里很内疚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reprehensible</td><td>/ˌreprɪˈhensəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>应受谴责的，可鄙的</td><td>morally reprehensible<br>道德上应受谴责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lying to the families was reprehensible and utterly unnecessary.  对那些家庭撒谎既可鄙又完全没有必要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sinful</td><td>/ˈsɪnfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有罪的，邪恶的</td><td>a sinful act<br>罪恶的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The preacher called greed the most sinful habit of our age.  牧师称贪婪是我们这个时代最罪恶的习性。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 reproachable</del></td><td>/rɪˈproʊtʃəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>该受责备的</td><td>reproachable carelessness<br>该受责备的粗心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing in her long record seemed reproachable to the inspectors.  在检查人员看来，她的长期记录中没有可责之处。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 inculpable</del></td><td>/ɪnˈkʌlpəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无罪的，无可指责的</td><td>entirely inculpable<br>完全无可指责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court declared the young driver inculpable in the accident.  法庭宣布这位年轻司机在事故中无过错。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 innocent</td><td>/ˈɪnəsənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的，无恶意的；无辜的</td><td>an innocent remark<br>无恶意的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 What began as an innocent joke ended in a serious quarrel.  一句本无恶意的玩笑最后引发了激烈争吵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>cultivate</b></td><td>/ˈkʌltɪveɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>提升，加强；种植，培养</td><td>cultivate a friendship<br>培养一段友谊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She cultivated a wide network of contacts during her years abroad.  她在国外的岁月里培养了广泛的人际关系网。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 educate</td><td>/ˈedʒəˌkeɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>教育，培养</td><td>educate the public<br>教育公众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The campaign educated farmers about safer ways to store grain.  这项活动教育农民如何更安全地储存粮食。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 instruct</td><td>/ɪnˈstrʌkt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>教导，指导；指示</td><td>instruct new employees<br>指导新员工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She instructed the class in basic first aid last week.  上周她教这个班基本的急救知识。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 illuminate</td><td>/ɪˈluːmɪneɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阐明</td><td>illuminate the problem<br>阐明问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her lecture illuminated a problem that had puzzled economists for years.  她的讲座阐明了一个困扰经济学家多年的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nurse</td><td>/ˈnɜːrs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>培育，照料；护士</td><td>nurse a grudge<br>心怀怨恨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He nursed the small business through three very difficult years.  他呵护这家小企业度过了三个极艰难的年头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grow</td><td>/ˈɡroʊ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>种植，生长；成长</td><td>grow vegetables<br>种蔬菜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They grow tomatoes and beans on a small rooftop garden.  他们在小小的屋顶花园里种番茄和豆子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 breed</td><td>/ˈbriːd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>引起，酿成；繁殖，养育</td><td>breed resentment<br>滋生怨恨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Secrecy in government breeds distrust among ordinary citizens.  政府的隐秘作风在普通民众中滋生不信任。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 produce</td><td>/prəˈduːs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>生产，制造；产品</td><td>produce steel locally<br>本地生产钢材</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The factory can produce ten thousand units of steel every month.  这家工厂每月能生产一万单位钢材。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 raise</td><td>/ˈreɪz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>提出（问题）；举起，提高</td><td>raise an objection<br>提出反对意见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He raised an objection just before the vote was taken.  就在投票前他提出了反对意见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 develop</td><td>/dɪˈveləp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>发展，培养</td><td>develop a skill<br>培养一项技能</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She developed a habit of reading for an hour each night.  她养成了每晚阅读一小时的习惯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nurture</td><td>/ˈnɜːrtʃər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>培育，培养</td><td>nurture young talent<br>培养年轻人才</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Good teachers nurture curiosity rather than simply rewarding correct answers.  好老师培育好奇心，而不只是奖励正确答案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 degrade</td><td>/dɪˈɡreɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>贬低，使降级；使退化</td><td>degrade the quality<br>降低质量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Such insulting jokes degrade everyone who repeats them.  这类侮辱性的笑话贬低了每个转述它们的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 deteriorate</td><td>/dɪˈtɪriəreɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>恶化，变坏</td><td>deteriorate rapidly<br>迅速恶化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her health deteriorated sharply during the cold winter.  在寒冷的冬天里她的健康急剧恶化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 impair</td><td>/ɪmˈper/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>损害，削弱</td><td>impair his judgment<br>削弱他的判断力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lack of sleep seriously impairs judgment and slows your reactions.  睡眠不足严重损害判断力并减慢反应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 balk</td><td>/bɔːk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词、及物动词</td><td>阻碍；不愿接受，拒绝</td><td>balk at the price<br>因价格而退缩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Buyers began to balk at the price after the third increase.  第三次涨价之后，买家开始因价格而退缩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 frustrate</td><td>/ˈfrʌstreɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阻挠，使受挫；使沮丧</td><td>frustrate one's plans<br>使计划受挫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy snow frustrated our plans to drive home.  大雪打乱了我们开车回家的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 thwart</td><td>/θwɔːrt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阻挠，挫败</td><td>thwart an attempt<br>挫败一次尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Alert guards thwarted the escape attempt.  警觉的守卫挫败了这次越狱企图。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>cumbersome</b></td><td>/ˈkʌmbərsəm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>笨重的，难处理的</td><td>a cumbersome procedure<br>繁琐的手续</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old permit system was so cumbersome that few farmers bothered applying.  旧的许可制度太繁琐，很少有农民愿意申请。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 unhandy</del></td><td>/ʌnˈhændi/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不便使用的；笨手笨脚的</td><td>an unhandy tool<br>不好用的工具</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This unhandy old ladder is difficult to carry upstairs.  这把不好用的旧梯子很难搬上楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ponderous</td><td>/ˈpɑːndərəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉重的</td><td>ponderous machinery<br>笨重的机器</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Ponderous machinery filled the workshop and shook the floor all day.  笨重的机器占满车间，整天震动地板。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heavy</td><td>/ˈhevi/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>重的，沉重的</td><td>too heavy to lift<br>重得抬不动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The box was so heavy that two men could barely move it.  这箱子太重，两个人几乎搬不动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unwieldy</td><td>/ʌnˈwiːldi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>笨重难用的；难管理的</td><td>an unwieldy bureaucracy<br>臃肿难管的官僚机构</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old camera was unwieldy but produced remarkably sharp pictures.  这台老相机笨重，却能拍出异常清晰的照片。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 onerous</td><td>/ˈɑːnərəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>繁重的，费力的</td><td>onerous responsibilities<br>繁重的责任</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Caring for the archive proved more onerous than the volunteers had expected.  照管这份档案比志愿者们预想的更费时费心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 thorny</td><td>/ˈθɔːrni/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>棘手的</td><td>a thorny problem<br>棘手的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The council postponed a thorny problem about school funding again.  议会又一次推迟了学校经费这个棘手问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 light</td><td>/ˈlaɪt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻微的，不严重的</td><td>a light meal<br>一顿便餐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He suffered only light injuries in the accident.  他在事故中只受了轻伤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 weightless</td><td>/ˈweɪtləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无重量的，失重的</td><td>feel almost weightless<br>感觉几乎失重</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Astronauts float weightless while the station circles the earth.  空间站绕地飞行时，航天员在失重中漂浮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>cunning</b></td><td>/ˈkʌnɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡猾的</td><td>a cunning scheme<br>狡猾的计谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fox was cunning enough to avoid every trap they set.  这只狐狸够狡猾，避开了他们设的每个陷阱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 artful</td><td>/ˈɑːrtfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>技艺高超的；狡猾的</td><td>an artful arrangement<br>巧妙的布置</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The chef's artful plating made the dish look like a painting.  厨师巧妙的摆盘让这道菜看起来像幅画。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crafty</td><td>/ˈkræfti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡猾的，诡计多端的</td><td>a crafty politician<br>狡猾的政客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A crafty lawyer found a loophole nobody else had noticed.  一位狡猾的律师找到了别人都没注意的漏洞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 devious</td><td>/ˈdiːviəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡猾的</td><td>devious tactics<br>狡诈的手段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He used devious means to obtain a copy of the secret contract.  他用狡猾的手段弄到了那份秘密合同的副本。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 foxy</td><td>/ˈfɑːksi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡猾的，机敏的</td><td>a foxy old trader<br>狡猾的老商人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The foxy old trader always knew when to sell everything.  这位狡猾的老商人总知道何时全部卖出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sly</td><td>/slaɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡猾的</td><td>a sly grin<br>狡黠的笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sly grin spread across his face as he hid the letter.  他藏起那封信时，脸上露出狡黠的笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tricky</td><td>/ˈtrɪki/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡猾的；棘手的</td><td>a tricky opponent<br>难对付的对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Parking here is tricky, especially during the busy evening hours.  这里停车很棘手，尤其在傍晚的繁忙时段。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wily</td><td>/ˈwaɪli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡诈的，狡猾的</td><td>a wily negotiator<br>狡猾的谈判者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A wily negotiator, he never revealed his lowest acceptable price.  作为狡猾的谈判者，他从不透露自己能接受的最低价。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 artless</td><td>/ˈɑrtləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺乏技巧的，笨拙的</td><td>an artless imitation<br>拙劣的模仿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting was an artless copy of a famous portrait.  这幅画是对一幅名作的拙劣临摹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 naive</td><td>/naɪˈiːv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>天真的，幼稚的</td><td>naive assumptions<br>天真的假设</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was naive to expect honesty from someone with that record.  指望有那种记录的人诚实太天真了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unsophisticated</td><td>/ˌʌnsəˈfɪstɪkeɪtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单纯的，不老练的</td><td>an unsophisticated buyer<br>不老练的买家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Unsophisticated investors are often the first to lose their money.  不老练的投资者往往最先赔钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>curmudgeon</b></td><td>/kɜːrˈmʌdʒən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>脾气坏的、爱抱怨的人</td><td>a grumpy old curmudgeon<br>脾气坏的老怪人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old curmudgeon next door complains about every child on the street.  隔壁那个爱抱怨的老怪人对街上每个孩子都有意见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 crosspatch</del></td><td>/ˈkrɔsˌpætʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>脾气坏的人，爱生气的人</td><td>a real crosspatch<br>十足的坏脾气鬼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The neighbors called him a crosspatch who complained about everything.  邻居说他是个什么都要抱怨的坏脾气鬼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 fusser</del></td><td>/ˈfʌsər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>爱挑剔抱怨的人</td><td>a hopeless fusser<br>没救的挑剔鬼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her uncle was a fusser who rearranged every dish on the table.  她叔叔是个挑剔鬼，把桌上每道菜都重摆一遍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 griper</del></td><td>/ˈɡraɪpər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>爱抱怨的人</td><td>a constant griper<br>整天抱怨的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Every office has one griper who is never satisfied.  每个办公室都有一个从不满足的抱怨鬼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 grouser</del></td><td>/ˈɡraʊzər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>爱牢骚的人</td><td>an old grouser<br>爱发牢骚的老家伙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old grouser muttered about the weather all afternoon long.  这位爱发牢骚的老人整个下午都在嘟囔天气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 growler</td><td>/ˈɡraʊlər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>咆哮的人，爱发火的人</td><td>a bad-tempered growler<br>脾气暴躁的家伙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our coach is a growler, but he genuinely cares about us.  我们教练爱吼人，但他真心关心我们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 grumbler</del></td><td>/ˈɡrʌmblər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>爱嘟囔抱怨的人</td><td>a habitual grumbler<br>习惯性抱怨的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The grumbler in the corner criticized every dish he was served.  角落里那个抱怨鬼把上的每道菜都批评一通。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 whiner</td><td>/ˈwaɪnər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>爱抱怨诉苦的人</td><td>a whiner on the team<br>队里爱抱怨的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nobody wants a whiner on a team facing this much pressure.  没人愿意在压力这么大的队里有个爱抱怨的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 agreeable person</td><td>/əˈɡriəbəl ˈpɜrsən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>和气可亲的人</td><td>a genuinely agreeable person<br>真正和气的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her father was an agreeable person whom everyone enjoyed visiting.  她父亲是个和气的人，人人都乐意去看他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>cursory</b></td><td>/ˈkɜːrsəri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>匆忙的，不注意细节的</td><td>a cursory glance<br>匆匆一瞥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A cursory inspection missed the cracks running along the bridge supports.  一次匆忙的检查漏掉了桥墩上的裂缝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 headlong</td><td>/ˈhedlɔːŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>鲁莽的，草率的</td><td>rush headlong into<br>贸然冲进…</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their headlong rush to publish left several errors in the final report.  他们急于发表的草率做法使最终报告留下了几处错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 overhasty</del></td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈheɪsti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过于匆忙的，草率的</td><td>an overhasty judgment<br>过于草率的判断</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their overhasty decision to expand nearly ruined the young company.  他们草率的扩张决定几乎毁了这家年轻公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pell-mell</td><td>/ˌpɛl ˈmɛl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>慌乱的，仓促混乱的</td><td>a pell-mell retreat<br>仓皇的撤退</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crowd rushed pell-mell toward the only open exit.  人群慌乱地涌向唯一开着的出口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 precipitate</td><td>/prɪˈsɪpɪteɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>匆忙的；促使，导致</td><td>a precipitate departure<br>匆忙的离去</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His precipitate resignation surprised colleagues who expected a much longer transition.  他匆忙的辞职让原以为过渡期会长得多的同事大吃一惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 precipitous</td><td>/prɪˈsɪpɪtəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常陡峭的；匆忙的</td><td>a precipitous decline<br>急剧的下滑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The path became precipitous near the summit, forcing us to use ropes.  靠近峰顶时山路变得非常陡峭，迫使我们用上绳索。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rash</td><td>/ræʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>草率的，仓促的</td><td>a rash decision<br>草率的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He regretted his rash decision to sell the house so quickly.  他后悔自己如此草率地决定迅速卖掉房子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fastidious</td><td>/fæˈstɪdiəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>挑剔的，极仔细的，追求完美的</td><td>fastidious about details<br>对细节极挑剔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is fastidious about details and rereads every line twice.  她对细节极为挑剔，每一行都要读两遍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 thorough</td><td>/ˈθɜːroʊ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>彻底的，详尽的</td><td>a thorough investigation<br>彻底的调查</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Inspectors carried out a thorough search of every storage room.  检查员对每间储藏室都做了彻底搜查。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 deliberate</td><td>/dɪˈlɪbərət/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深思熟虑的，故意的</td><td>a deliberate attempt<br>故意的尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The delay was deliberate, designed to weaken our position.  这次延误是故意的，目的是削弱我们的立场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unhurried</td><td>/ʌnˈhɜːrid/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>从容的，不慌忙的</td><td>an unhurried pace<br>从容的步调</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He works in an unhurried way but never misses a deadline.  他做事从容，却从不误期。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unrushed</del></td><td>/ʌnˈrʌʃt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不急促的，从容的</td><td>an unrushed conversation<br>不慌不忙的交谈</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We enjoyed a long, unrushed breakfast on the quiet terrace.  我们在安静的露台上享受了一顿悠长从容的早餐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>curt</b></td><td>/kɜːrt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>言词简略的，直接（以至显得粗鲁）的</td><td>a curt answer<br>简慢的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her curt reply made it clear that the discussion was over.  她简慢的回答清楚表明讨论已经结束。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abrupt</td><td>/əˈbrʌpt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>突然的；言语生硬的</td><td>an abrupt reply<br>生硬的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His abrupt answer ended the conversation before it really began.  他生硬的回答让谈话还没开始就结束了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bluff</td><td>/blʌf/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>虚张声势地骗人，唬人</td><td>bluff your way through<br>靠虚张声势混过去</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tried to bluff his way through the whole interview.  他试图靠虚张声势混过整场面试。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brusque</td><td>/brʌsk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>唐突无礼的，简慢的</td><td>a brusque manner<br>唐突的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clerk's brusque manner offended nearly every waiting customer.  那名职员唐突的态度几乎冒犯了所有等候的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 downright</td><td>/ˈdaʊnˌraɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>完全的，彻底的</td><td>downright dangerous<br>十足危险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That suggestion is downright dangerous and should be dropped.  那个建议完全是危险的，应该放弃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unceremonious</td><td>/ˌʌnserəˈmoʊniəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>唐突无礼的，草率的</td><td>an unceremonious dismissal<br>唐突的解职</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His unceremonious exit surprised the guests still waiting for dessert.  他唐突离场，让还等着甜点的客人吃了一惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 circuitous</td><td>/sərˈkjuːɪtəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迂回的，不直接的</td><td>a circuitous route<br>迂回的路线</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We took a circuitous route to avoid the flooded highway.  我们绕了一条迂回的路以避开被淹的公路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 mealymouthed</del></td><td>/ˈmiliˌmaʊðd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>说话含糊委婉的</td><td>a mealymouthed apology<br>含糊其辞的道歉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Voters were tired of mealymouthed answers about the missing money.  选民厌倦了关于失踪款项含糊其辞的回答。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 3

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>curtail</b></td><td>/kɜːrˈteɪl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>缩短，削减</td><td>curtail public spending<br>削减公共开支</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Bad weather curtailed our trip by three days.  坏天气使我们的旅程缩短了三天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abbreviate</td><td>/əˈbriːvieɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>缩写，缩短</td><td>abbreviate a long title<br>缩写长标题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 You may abbreviate 'Doctor' to 'Dr.' on the envelope.  信封上可以把 Doctor 缩写为 Dr.。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abridge</td><td>/əˈbrɪdʒ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>缩短，删节</td><td>an abridged edition<br>删节版</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The publisher abridged the long novel for younger readers.  出版商为年轻读者删节了这部长篇小说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 syncopate</del></td><td>/ˈsɪŋkəˌpeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>省略词中音节；使切分节奏</td><td>a syncopated rhythm<br>切分节奏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Jazz players often syncopate the beat to create tension.  爵士乐手常把节拍切分以制造张力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 truncate</td><td>/ˈtrʌŋkeɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>截短，缩短</td><td>truncate a long sentence<br>截短长句</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor truncated the article to fit the page.  编辑把文章截短以适应版面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cut back</td><td>/kʌt ˈbæk/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>削减，缩减</td><td>cut back on sugar<br>减少吃糖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We had to cut back on spending after the pay cut.  减薪后我们不得不削减开支。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 elongate</td><td>/ɪˈlɔːŋɡeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>拉长，延长</td><td>elongate the shape<br>拉长形状</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Tall mirrors elongate the room and make it look larger.  高镜子拉长了房间，使它显得更大。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 protract</del></td><td>/prəˈtrækt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>延长，拖长</td><td>protract the negotiations<br>拖长谈判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Endless objections protracted the meeting into the evening.  无休止的反对把会议拖到了晚上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prolong</td><td>/prəˈlɔːŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>延长，拖延</td><td>prolong someone's life<br>延长某人寿命</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New treatments can prolong life by several years.  新疗法能把生命延长数年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extend</td><td>/ɪkˈstend/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>延长，扩展</td><td>extend a deadline<br>延长截止期限</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They agreed to extend the deadline by two weeks.  他们同意把截止日期延长两周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lengthen</td><td>/ˈleŋθən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>变长，加长</td><td>lengthen a skirt<br>把裙子放长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The days lengthen noticeably after the spring equinox.  春分之后白天明显变长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>cynic</b></td><td>/ˈsɪnɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>愤世嫉俗者</td><td>a hardened cynic<br>顽固的愤世者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a hardened cynic could doubt the sincerity of her apology.  只有顽固的愤世者才会怀疑她道歉的诚意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 misanthrope</td><td>/ˈmɪsənˌθroʊp/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>厌恶人类的人</td><td>a bitter misanthrope<br>满怀怨恨的厌世者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old misanthrope lived alone and refused every friendly invitation.  这位厌世的老人独居，拒绝一切友好的邀请。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 naysayer</td><td>/ˈneɪseɪər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>习惯唱反调的人</td><td>ignore the naysayers<br>无视唱反调的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The naysayers insisted the bridge could never be built here.  唱反调的人坚持说这里绝不可能建桥。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pessimist</td><td>/ˈpesəməst/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>悲观主义者</td><td>a lifelong pessimist<br>一生的悲观者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even the pessimists admitted that the harvest looked unusually good.  连悲观者也承认收成看起来格外好。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dabble</b></td><td>/ˈdæbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>涉猎，对…浅尝辄止</td><td>dabble in painting<br>涉猎绘画</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He dabbled in photography for a year and then lost all interest.  他涉猎摄影一年，之后就完全失去了兴趣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dedicate</td><td>/ˈdedəˌkeɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>投身于，献身</td><td>dedicate oneself to research<br>投身研究</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She dedicated twenty years to studying a single kind of beetle.  她用二十年只研究一种甲虫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 specialize</td><td>/ˈspeʃəˌlaɪz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>专门研究，专攻</td><td>specialize in tax law<br>专攻税法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He specializes in restoring clocks made before the last century.  他专门修复上世纪之前制造的钟表。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 specialist</td><td>/ˈspeʃələst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>专家，专门研究者</td><td>consult a specialist<br>咨询专家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her doctor sent her to a specialist in rare blood diseases.  医生把她转诊给罕见血液病专家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>daft</b></td><td>/dæft/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的，傻的</td><td>a daft suggestion<br>愚蠢的建议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Selling the only truck in winter was a truly daft decision.  在冬天卖掉唯一的卡车实在是个愚蠢的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fatuous</td><td>/ˈfætʃuəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚笨的，昏庸的</td><td>a fatuous remark<br>愚蠢的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His fatuous comment showed he had not read the report.  他愚蠢的评论说明他没读过那份报告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 featherheaded</del></td><td>/ˈfɛðərˌhɛdɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻浮愚笨的</td><td>a featherheaded plan<br>轻率愚蠢的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a featherheaded manager would sign such a vague contract.  只有轻浮愚笨的经理才会签这么含糊的合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nonsensical</td><td>/nɑːnˈsensɪkəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>荒谬的，无意义的</td><td>a nonsensical argument<br>荒谬的论点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The instructions were nonsensical and helped nobody.  说明荒谬无理，对谁都没帮助。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 preposterous</td><td>/prɪˈpɑːstərəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>荒谬的，极其反常的</td><td>a preposterous claim<br>荒唐的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The idea that he acted alone is preposterous.  说他独自行动的说法荒谬至极。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 senseless</td><td>/ˈsensləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无意义的，愚蠢的</td><td>senseless violence<br>毫无意义的暴力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cutting the safety budget now would be completely senseless.  现在削减安全预算完全是愚蠢之举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 judicious</td><td>/dʒuˈdɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的，慎重的</td><td>a judicious choice<br>明智的选择</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A judicious use of savings carried the family through two difficult years.  明智地使用积蓄让这家人度过了两个艰难的年头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prudent</td><td>/ˈpruːdnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的；小心谨慎的，审慎的</td><td>a prudent investment<br>一笔审慎的投资</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It would be prudent to save part of every paycheck.  每份工资都存下一部分才是明智之举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sagacious</td><td>/səˈɡeɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>睿智的，有远见的</td><td>a sagacious adviser<br>睿智的顾问</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His sagacious advice saved the family business from certain ruin.  他睿智的建议救家族生意于必败之境。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sapient</td><td>/ˈseɪpiənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪明的，有洞察力的</td><td>a sapient observation<br>有洞察力的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A few sapient remarks from the editor saved the entire chapter.  编辑几句有洞察力的话救了整整一章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wise</td><td>/ˈwaɪz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的，有智慧的</td><td>a wise decision<br>明智的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was wise of her to save money before quitting the job.  她辞职前先存钱，很明智。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dazzling</b></td><td>/ˈdæzlɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>炫目的，耀眼的</td><td>a dazzling display<br>炫目的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dancers wore dazzling costumes that glittered under the stage lights.  舞者们穿着在舞台灯光下闪耀的炫目服装。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 beaming</td><td>/ˈbiːmɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>光芒四射的；笑容满面的</td><td>a beaming smile<br>灿烂的笑容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She greeted us with a beaming smile at the airport gate.  她在机场门口用灿烂的笑容迎接我们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 candescent</del></td><td>/kænˈdɛsənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>白热发光的</td><td>candescent metal<br>白热的金属</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The candescent iron glowed orange as the smith hammered it.  铁匠敲打时，白热的铁块发出橙光。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bright</td><td>/ˈbraɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>快活的，开朗的；明亮的</td><td>a bright smile<br>开朗的笑容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She greeted every customer with a bright and genuine smile.  她用开朗真诚的笑容迎接每位顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 effulgent</td><td>/ɪˈfʌldʒənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>光辉灿烂的</td><td>an effulgent sunrise<br>灿烂的日出</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An effulgent sunrise turned the snow on the eastern ridges pale gold.  灿烂的日出把东侧山脊上的积雪染成浅金色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glowing</td><td>/ˈɡloʊɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>发光的，炽热的</td><td>glowing embers<br>发光的余烬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Glowing coals gave the little room a warm orange light.  发光的炭火给小屋带来温暖的橙色光线。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 incandescent</td><td>/ˌɪnkænˈdesənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>白热的，耀眼的</td><td>an incandescent bulb<br>白炽灯泡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The incandescent filament burned out after only a few weeks.  白炽灯丝仅用几周就烧断了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 luminous</td><td>/ˈluːmɪnəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>发光的，明亮的</td><td>a luminous dial<br>夜光表盘</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The luminous dial of his watch glowed faintly in the dark.  他手表的夜光表盘在黑暗中微微发亮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 refulgent</del></td><td>/rɪˈfʌldʒənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>辉煌的，灿烂的</td><td>refulgent golden domes<br>灿烂的金色圆顶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Refulgent golden domes rose above the rooftops of the old city.  灿烂的金色圆顶耸立在老城的屋顶之上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shining</td><td>/ˈʃaɪnɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>闪亮的，发光的</td><td>shining armor<br>闪亮的铠甲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her shining eyes showed how much the praise meant to her.  她发亮的眼睛显示出这份赞扬对她多重要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dim</td><td>/ˈdɪm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>暗淡的，模糊的</td><td>a dim light<br>昏暗的灯光</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The room was too dim for reading anything but large print.  房间太暗，只能看大字。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dull</td><td>/dʌl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迟钝的；乏味的</td><td>a dull student<br>迟钝的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lecture was so dull that half the class slept.  讲座乏味得让一半学生睡着了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lackluster</td><td>/ˈlæklʌstər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>黯淡无光泽的</td><td>a lackluster performance<br>平淡无光的表现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her lackluster hair and tired face worried everyone at home.  她黯淡的头发和疲惫的脸让家人都担心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unbright</del></td><td>/ʌnˈbraɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明亮的，暗淡的</td><td>an unbright morning<br>并不明亮的早晨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unbright winter sky made the whole town look grey.  暗淡的冬日天空让整座小镇显得灰蒙蒙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unbrilliant</del></td><td>/ʌnˈbrɪljənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不耀眼的，平庸的</td><td>an unbrilliant result<br>并不出色的结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His unbrilliant but steady work earned him quiet respect.  他不出彩却稳健的工作赢得了默默的尊重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dally</b></td><td>/ˈdæli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>虚度时光，磨蹭</td><td>dally over lunch<br>吃午饭磨蹭半天</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Don't dally on the way home; dinner is almost ready.  回家路上别磨蹭，晚饭快好了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dawdle</td><td>/ˈdɔːdl/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>拖拖拉拉，磨蹭</td><td>dawdle on the way<br>路上拖拖拉拉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She dawdled over her homework until it was nearly midnight.  她做作业拖拖拉拉，一直到近午夜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 loll</td><td>/lɑːl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>懒躺，闲混度日</td><td>loll on the sofa<br>懒躺在沙发上</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children lolled on the sofa instead of finishing their homework.  孩子们懒躺在沙发上，而不是把作业做完。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lounge</td><td>/ˈlaʊndʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>懒散地待着，闲荡</td><td>lounge by the pool<br>在泳池边闲躺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They lounged in the shade all afternoon without doing anything.  他们整个下午在阴凉处闲躺，什么也没做。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hang around</td><td>/hæŋ əˈraʊnd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>闲荡，无所事事地待着</td><td>hang around the station<br>在车站附近闲荡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They hung around the gate for hours waiting for the singer.  他们在门口闲等了几个小时，等那位歌手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>damper</b></td><td>/ˈdæmpər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>抑制因素，扫兴的事物</td><td>put a damper on<br>给…泼冷水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rain put a damper on our plans for the picnic.  这场雨给我们的野餐计划泼了冷水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 ameliorator</del></td><td>/əˈmiljəˌreɪtər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>改善者，缓和者</td><td>an ameliorator of suffering<br>苦难的缓解者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cheap medicine became a great ameliorator of rural poverty here.  廉价药物成为这里农村贫困的重要缓解因素。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dandy</b></td><td>/ˈdændi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>纨绔子弟，爱打扮的人</td><td>dressed like a dandy<br>打扮得像纨绔子弟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young dandy spent more on silk shirts than on rent.  那位年轻的纨绔子弟买丝绸衬衫的钱比房租还多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fop</td><td>/fɑːp/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>爱打扮的男子，花花公子</td><td>a vain young fop<br>虚荣的年轻花花公子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fop spent more on coats than on food each month.  这位花花公子每月买外套的钱多于吃饭的钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gallant</td><td>/ˈɡælənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>英勇的</td><td>a gallant defense<br>英勇的防守</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The gallant paratroopers jumped from the plane without a moment's hesitation.  英勇的伞兵们毫不犹豫地跳出了飞机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dank</b></td><td>/dæŋk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>阴湿的</td><td>a dank cellar<br>阴湿的地窖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They stored the wine in a dank cellar beneath the kitchen.  他们把酒存放在厨房下面阴湿的地窖里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 damp</td><td>/ˈdæmp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>抑制，使沮丧；潮湿的</td><td>damp their enthusiasm<br>打消他们的热情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rain did not damp our enthusiasm for the trip.  下雨并没有打消我们对这次旅行的热情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 wettish</del></td><td>/ˈwɛtɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有点潮湿的</td><td>wettish soil<br>略湿的土壤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The towels were still wettish when we packed them away.  我们收起毛巾时它们还有点湿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dapper</b></td><td>/ˈdæpər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>衣冠整洁的</td><td>a dapper little man<br>衣着整洁的小个子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A dapper old gentleman in a gray suit greeted us at the door.  一位身穿灰色西装、衣冠整洁的老先生在门口迎接我们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 natty</td><td>/ˈnæti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>整洁的，时髦的</td><td>a natty blue suit<br>时髦的蓝西装</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He arrived at the reception in a natty blue suit and polished shoes.  他穿着时髦的蓝西装和擦亮的皮鞋来到招待会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sharp</td><td>/ˈʃɑːrp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>尖刻的；锋利的；敏锐的</td><td>a sharp tongue<br>说话尖刻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The critic has a sharp tongue but an unusually fair mind.  这位评论家言辞尖刻，心思却格外公正。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 snappy</td><td>/ˈsnæpi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>时髦利落的；简洁的</td><td>a snappy jacket<br>利落时髦的外套</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He arrived in a snappy grey suit and polished shoes.  他穿着利落的灰西装和擦亮的皮鞋来了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spruce</td><td>/spruːs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>整洁漂亮的；打扮整洁</td><td>look neat and spruce<br>显得整洁利落</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old waiter always looked spruce despite his worn uniform.  尽管制服磨旧，这位老侍者总是显得整洁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 frowsy</del></td><td>/ˈfraʊzi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>邋遢的</td><td>a frowsy old coat<br>邋遢的旧外套</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He looked frowsy after sleeping two nights on the train.  在火车上睡了两夜后，他显得很邋遢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unkempt</td><td>/ˌʌnˈkempt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不整洁的，蓬乱的</td><td>unkempt hair<br>蓬乱的头发</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He arrived at the interview with unkempt hair and a wrinkled shirt.  他去面试时头发蓬乱，衬衫也皱皱巴巴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 slovenly</td><td>/ˈslʌvnli/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>邋遢的，不整洁的</td><td>slovenly appearance<br>邋遢的外表</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His slovenly desk made a bad impression on every visitor.  他邋遢的办公桌给每位来访者留下坏印象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 4

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dappled</b></td><td>/ˈdæpld/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有斑点的，斑驳的</td><td>dappled sunlight<br>斑驳的阳光</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Dappled light fell through the leaves onto the quiet forest path.  斑驳的光透过树叶洒在幽静的林间小路上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blotchy</td><td>/ˈblɑːtʃi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有斑块的，一片片的</td><td>blotchy red skin<br>红斑块的皮肤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her face turned blotchy after an hour in the cold wind.  在冷风中待了一小时后，她的脸起了红斑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mottled</td><td>/ˈmɑːtəld/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有杂色斑点的</td><td>mottled green leaves<br>带杂色斑的绿叶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mottled stone floor hid the stains left by rain.  杂色斑驳的石地板藏住了雨水留下的污痕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 specked</del></td><td>/spɛkt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有小斑点的</td><td>specked with paint<br>沾着点点油漆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His glasses were specked with white paint after the long day.  忙了一整天后，他的眼镜上沾满白漆点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 piebald</td><td>/ˈpaɪbɔːld/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有黑白斑块的</td><td>a piebald horse<br>黑白花的马</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A piebald horse grazed quietly beside the wooden fence.  一匹黑白花的马在木栅栏旁静静吃草。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 splotched</del></td><td>/splɑtʃt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有大片污斑的</td><td>splotched with ink<br>沾着墨渍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The letter was splotched with ink and hard to read.  这封信沾满墨渍，很难辨读。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stippled</td><td>/ˈstɪpəld/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>点画成的，布满小点的</td><td>a stippled drawing<br>点画的素描</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The artist's stippled portrait used thousands of tiny black dots.  这位画家的点画肖像用了数千个小黑点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spotted</td><td>/ˈspɑːtɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有斑点的</td><td>a spotted dog<br>有斑点的狗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A spotted deer stepped quietly out of the morning mist.  一头有斑点的鹿静静走出晨雾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unspotted</td><td>/ʌnˈspɑtɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无斑点的，洁净的</td><td>an unspotted white cloth<br>洁白无斑的布</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She kept the tablecloth unspotted through the entire noisy dinner.  整场喧闹的晚餐里她都让桌布保持洁净。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>daredevil</b></td><td>/ˈderdevl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>大胆鲁莽的（人）</td><td>a daredevil stunt<br>一次鲁莽的特技</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The daredevil rider jumped his motorcycle over six parked cars.  那位鲁莽大胆的骑手驾摩托车跃过六辆停放的汽车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 audacious</td><td>/ɔːˈdeɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>大胆的，（但往往）愚勇的；令人吃惊的</td><td>an audacious plan<br>大胆的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The general approved an audacious plan to cross the river at night.  将军批准了一个夜间渡河的大胆计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brash</td><td>/bræʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚勇的，鲁莽的</td><td>a brash young executive<br>鲁莽的年轻主管</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His brash remarks offended nearly everyone at the dinner table.  他鲁莽的言论几乎冒犯了餐桌上的每个人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 madcap</td><td>/ˈmædkæp/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>莽撞冲动的；莽撞冲动的人</td><td>a madcap adventure<br>一场冒险的疯狂之旅</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a madcap would try to cross that river during the flood.  只有热爱冒险的疯子才会在洪水期试图渡过那条河。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 overbold</del></td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈboʊld/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过于大胆的，鲁莽的</td><td>an overbold attack<br>过于冒险的进攻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His overbold plan impressed nobody on the cautious old committee.  他过于冒险的计划没打动谨慎的老委员会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reckless</td><td>/ˈrekləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不考虑后果的，大胆鲁莽的</td><td>reckless driving<br>鲁莽驾驶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His reckless driving on the icy road caused a serious accident.  他在结冰路面上的鲁莽驾驶造成了一场严重事故。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 temerarious</del></td><td>/ˌtɛməˈrɛriəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>鲁莽的，胆大妄为的</td><td>a temerarious decision<br>一个鲁莽的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was temerarious of him to sail alone in that storm.  他在那场风暴中独自出海实在鲁莽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 circumspect</td><td>/ˈsɜːrkəmspekt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谨慎的，小心的</td><td>remain circumspect in public<br>在公开场合保持谨慎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Diplomats stay circumspect in public and blunt behind closed doors.  外交官在公开场合谨慎，关起门来则直言不讳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 guarded</td><td>/ˈɡɑːrdəd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谨慎的，有保留的</td><td>a guarded response<br>有保留的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister gave a guarded welcome to the new proposal.  部长对新提案表示了有保留的欢迎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 heedful</del></td><td>/ˈhiːdfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>留心的，注意的</td><td>heedful of the warning<br>留意警告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heedful of the forecast, the climbers turned back early.  留意到天气预报，登山者提早返回了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prudent</td><td>/ˈpruːdnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的；小心谨慎的，审慎的</td><td>a prudent investment<br>一笔审慎的投资</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It would be prudent to save part of every paycheck.  每份工资都存下一部分才是明智之举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wary</td><td>/ˈweri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>警惕的，谨慎的</td><td>wary of strangers<br>对陌生人警惕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Children are taught to be wary of strangers offering free rides.  孩子们被教导要警惕主动提供搭车的陌生人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dart</b></td><td>/dɑːrt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>猛冲，突然移动</td><td>dart across the road<br>猛冲过马路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A squirrel darted across the lawn and vanished up the old oak.  一只松鼠猛冲过草坪，蹿上老橡树不见了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flitter</td><td>/ˈflɪtər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>掠飞，飞舞</td><td>flitter among the flowers<br>在花丛间飞舞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Butterflies flitter among the wildflowers every summer in the sunny meadow.  每年夏天蝴蝶都在阳光下的草地野花间飞舞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flutter</td><td>/ˈflʌtər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>飘动；快速拍翅</td><td>flutter in the breeze<br>在微风中飘动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The flags flutter in the breeze above the crowded stadium entrance.  旗帜在拥挤的体育场入口上方随微风飘动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 affront</td><td>/əˈfrʌnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>侮辱，冒犯</td><td>an affront to dignity<br>对尊严的侮辱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His careless remark was an affront to everyone in the room.  他随口的一句话侮辱了房间里的每个人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 barb</td><td>/bɑːrb/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>尖锐而严厉的批评</td><td>a sharp barb<br>尖刻的讥讽</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her final barb about his cooking silenced the whole table.  她最后那句关于他厨艺的尖刻讥讽让整桌人都不说话了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 offense</td><td>/əˈfens/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>冒犯，得罪；犯罪</td><td>take offense at<br>因……而生气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She took offense at his joke about her accent.  她因他拿她的口音开玩笑而生气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sarcasm</td><td>/ˈsɑːrkæzəm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>讽刺，轻蔑</td><td>a hint of sarcasm<br>一丝讽刺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was a faint note of sarcasm in his polite reply.  他礼貌的回答中带着一丝讽刺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slight</td><td>/slaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>轻微的，不重要的；轻视，怠慢</td><td>slight her contribution<br>轻视她的贡献</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt slighted when nobody mentioned his contribution during the ceremony.  典礼上无人提及他的贡献，他觉得受到了轻视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slur</td><td>/slɜːr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>含糊不清地说；诋毁，污辱</td><td>slur one's words<br>说话含糊不清</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was so tired that he began to slur his words.  他累得开始说话含糊不清。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>daunt</b></td><td>/dɔːnt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使胆怯，吓倒</td><td>daunt the bravest soldiers<br>吓倒最勇敢的士兵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The steep climb ahead did not daunt the experienced mountain guide.  前方陡峭的攀登丝毫没有吓倒这位经验丰富的山地导游。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 demoralize</td><td>/dɪˈmɔːrəlaɪz/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使士气低落；贬低，使堕落</td><td>demoralize the troops<br>使部队士气低落</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Repeated pay cuts demoralized the staff more than the long hours.  反复减薪比长时间工作更让员工士气低落。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dishearten</td><td>/dɪsˈhɑːrtn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使沮丧，使失去信心</td><td>disheartened by the news<br>因这消息而沮丧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The string of early losses disheartened even the most loyal fans.  开局连输让最忠实的球迷也失去了信心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dismay</td><td>/dɪsˈmeɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使失去勇气；使不安，使焦虑</td><td>dismay his supporters<br>使支持者沮丧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The abrupt policy reversal dismayed his supporters and delighted his rivals.  政策的突然反转让他的支持者沮丧，让对手高兴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 dispirit</del></td><td>/dɪˈspɪrɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使沮丧，使气馁</td><td>dispirit the whole team<br>使全队士气低落</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The endless rain dispirited even the most cheerful campers.  连绵的阴雨让最开朗的营员也沮丧起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frustrate</td><td>/ˈfrʌstreɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阻挠，使受挫；使沮丧</td><td>frustrate one's plans<br>使计划受挫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy snow frustrated our plans to drive home.  大雪打乱了我们开车回家的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unnerve</td><td>/ʌnˈnɜːrv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使紧张不安，使气馁</td><td>unnerve the young witness<br>使年轻证人紧张不安</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sudden silence in the courtroom unnerved the young witness completely.  法庭上突然的寂静让年轻证人完全紧张起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 embolden</td><td>/ɪmˈboʊldən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>鼓励，使大胆</td><td>embolden the rebels<br>壮了叛乱者的胆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Early success emboldened her to ask for a promotion.  初期的成功让她有胆量要求升职。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 make resolute</td><td>/meɪk ˈrɛzəluːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使坚定，使果断</td><td>make resolute in purpose<br>使目标坚定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of hardship made him resolute in pursuing his dream.  多年的艰辛使他在追求梦想上变得坚定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hearten</td><td>/ˈhɑːrtn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>给予鼓励，鼓舞</td><td>hearten the troops<br>鼓舞部队士气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crowd's cheering heartened the runners on the last steep hill.  观众的欢呼在最后一个陡坡上鼓舞了跑者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pusillanimous</td><td>/ˌpjuːsɪˈlænɪməs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>胆小的，懦弱的</td><td>a pusillanimous response<br>软弱的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics called the government's pusillanimous silence a betrayal of victims.  批评者称政府懦弱的沉默是对受害者的背叛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 trepid</del></td><td>/ˈtrɛpɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>胆怯的，惊恐的</td><td>a trepid first step<br>胆怯的第一步</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She took a trepid step onto the swaying rope bridge.  她胆怯地踏上摇晃的绳桥。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 craven</td><td>/ˈkreɪvn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常懦弱的，因胆小而遭人鄙视的</td><td>a craven retreat<br>怯懦的撤退</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His craven silence during the trial disappointed everyone who had trusted him.  他在庭审中懦弱的沉默让所有信任过他的人失望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 easily discouraged</td><td>/ˈiːzəli dɪsˈkʌrɪdʒd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>容易气馁的</td><td>easily discouraged by criticism<br>易因批评而气馁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Beginners are easily discouraged when their first attempts fail badly.  初学者在最初的尝试严重失败时容易气馁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 meek</td><td>/miːk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>顺从的</td><td>a meek and quiet manner<br>温顺安静的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Too meek to complain, he accepted the extra work without saying a word.  他太顺从了，不敢抱怨，一句话没说就接下了额外的工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 timorous</td><td>/ˈtɪmərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>胆小的</td><td>a timorous little voice<br>胆怯的小声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The timorous student finally raised her hand to answer the question.  那个胆怯的学生终于举手回答问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dawdle</b></td><td>/ˈdɔːdl/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>拖拖拉拉，磨蹭</td><td>dawdle on the way<br>路上拖拖拉拉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She dawdled over her homework until it was nearly midnight.  她做作业拖拖拉拉，一直到近午夜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crawl</td><td>/ˈkrɔːl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>缓慢行进；爬行</td><td>crawl along the highway<br>在公路上缓慢行进</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Traffic crawled along the flooded highway for nearly three hours.  车流在被淹的公路上缓慢行进了近三个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 creep</td><td>/kriːp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>缓慢地行进</td><td>creep along slowly<br>缓慢地爬行前进</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Traffic crept forward only a few meters at a time all morning.  整个上午车流每次只缓慢前进几米。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dally</td><td>/ˈdæli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>虚度时光，磨蹭</td><td>dally over lunch<br>吃午饭磨蹭半天</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Don't dally on the way home; dinner is almost ready.  回家路上别磨蹭，晚饭快好了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 dillydally</del></td><td>/ˈdɪlidæli/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>磨蹭，浪费时间</td><td>dillydally on the way<br>在路上磨蹭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Stop dillydallying and finish your homework before dinner is served.  别磨蹭了，晚饭前把作业写完。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lag</td><td>/læɡ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>落后，行动缓慢</td><td>lag behind the group<br>落在队伍后面</td></tr>
